## 2. Chapter Purpose

**Why this chapter exists:**  
THE NOVEL'S PIVOTAL SCENE. Shankarappa speaks the 1962 shared-use terms calmly and specifically — framed as **held/transmitted memory**, not eyewitness testimony. Both fathers flinch — because both are partly wrong. The path was never exclusively anyone's. The terms were: shared use, rotating maintenance, neither family builds permanent structures that narrow it. Both sides violated aspects. Neither can claim victim status purely.

Meera **chooses to stay and attend** this meeting (ISS-02) — she could retreat after Ch15 humiliation but doesn't. This is her agency beat.

**Removal test (what breaks if deleted):**  
Novel has no pivot; the dispute has no truth; Shankarappa never demonstrates his value; the central argument (that some memories are necessary and costly to carry) never gets its primary scene; the entire architecture of reveals 2–8 loses its anchor.

**Promise job:**  
P-1962 opens (the terms now exist in the novel's active texture). P-GUEST dramatically advances (he's not just a dull guest — he KNOWS things). But P-GUEST doesn't close (reader still can't explain HOW he knows or what category of person he is).

**Emotional job:**  
Disappointment. Not the warm kind — the cold kind. When you learn the truth and it doesn't vindicate you. When both sides are diminished by accuracy.

**Institutional / RP job:**  
**3 RP — the novel's single largest spend.** R2 from Reveal Map. On-page: Shankarappa speaks specific 1962 terms (date, arrangement, who agreed, what was shared). Reader interprets: "He somehow knows real history." Incorrect interpretation: "He has secret documents / is a retired revenue officer." Correct (writer only, unstated): Holder memory + legitimacy via disappointment.

---

## 3. Opening State

**POV entering emotion:**  
Apprehensive. Both families called together again (Lakshmi brokered this through both grandmothers). Meera present because she **chooses to stay** (post-Ch15 failure — she can't retreat without becoming the cousin who only visits for weddings). She doesn't expect anything specific.

**POV entering knowledge:**  
Meera knows: her mediation failed (Ch15); the dispute is getting worse; families are here to "try again"; Shankarappa is that quiet guest from wedding-eve (Ch12); she doesn't know what he knows or why he's present at this meeting.

**Other characters — entering emotions:**
- Shankarappa: heavy; deliberate; this is what he's been assessing — whether to speak. He has decided: yes. The cost is his comfort. (Writer only.)
- Ramesh: stiff pride; seated; expects nothing good
- Venki: defensive; seated opposite; similar
- Lakshmi: watchful; she knows what's about to happen (she's seen his type before)
- Other family members: background seats; watching

**Unresolved pressure entering:**  
All Act I + II threads. Failed mediation (Ch15). Drunk boast (Ch14). Police file (Ch11). Wedding complete but poisoned (Ch13–14). P-GUEST unanswered.

**Reader assumptions currently true:**  
The dispute might be intractable; nobody seems to know the real history; maybe law will have to decide.

**Reader assumptions we will not correct this chapter:**  
How Shankarappa knows what he knows. (Reader will suspect documents / official role / local historian — all wrong. The HOW stays mysterious.)

Back and forth. Familiar. Going nowhere. Meera watches. Then: a pause. Ramesh says to the room generally: "Let anyone prove when it was different." It's a challenge to the air. A dare that expects no answer. |

|-------|-------|
| Location | Same courtyard — continuous |
| Time of day | 5:00 p.m. → 6:00 p.m. |
| Characters onstage | All from S01 — focused now on Shankarappa speaking |
| Offstage pressure | Everything hanging on what he says |
| Goal (Shankarappa's — perceived by Meera) | Speak the truth as he holds it; without victory or vengeance; let it land however it lands |
| Conflict / obstacle | Shankarappa speaks. Calm. Specific. Not loud. **Attribution lock (ISS-10):** He does NOT say "I was there," "I saw," "I remember when they sat." Terms are spoken as **held/transmitted memory** — Ch25 model. Paraphrased in outline (prose will render precisely): "What was agreed in 1962 — what Mallappa Hegde and Chandrappa Gowda held between them under the tamarind, before it fell in the '88 storm — was this: both families use the path. Maintenance: alternating years — odd years Hegde clears brush; even years Gowda fills potholes. Neither builds permanent structure that narrows the walking width below a loaded cart. What Mallappa held as his word was: 'Let the path be wider than our pride.' Chandrappa held: 'And let neither of us need a document for what our sons should remember.'" — He stops. The silence is enormous. Both Ramesh and Venki are pale. Because: Ramesh's "our path" was wrong — it was always shared. And Venki's claim that Hegde-side narrowed first — also wrong: the wire (Gowda-side) narrowed it below cart-width. BOTH are wrong. BOTH flinch. Neither gets the vindication they wanted. |

He simply stated what was held. The specificity (tamarind tree, storm, names, exact phrasing) is undeniable. The fathers cannot say "that's wrong" because the details are too precise to be invented and too old to be recently manufactured. They can only be: received. The room is silent. |
| Exit image | Silence after specific sentences. No one moves. Shankarappa sits back. Meera's observation: his face shows no triumph. If anything: exhaustion. He did not enjoy that. |

| Continuity outputs | 1962 terms are now in the room; both sides wrong (partially); Shankarappa demonstrated: precise historical memory + willingness to speak it without stake; reader knows he KNOWS; reader doesn't know HOW/WHY; P-1962 open and devastating; P-GUEST advanced massively; Meera's worldview shifted (someone here has authority she doesn't understand) |

His legitimacy comes from: precision + no stake + willingness to disappoint both sides + long presence without extraction. Ch25 will model explicit uncertainty ("what I was told"). Ch16 speaks with held-memory authority without eyewitness claim.

## 10. Drafting Constraints

**Prose must avoid:**  
Making Shankarappa dramatic/cinematic; orchestral narrator build-up; "the room held its breath" clichés; explaining to reader what just happened; making either father a villain in their reaction; opening on mystery before human logistics (ISS-07).

**Forbidden exposition:**  
How Shankarappa knows; institutional explanation; canon background; what kind of organization exists; any framing of this as "reveal."

**Forbidden lore:**  
All labels. All structure. All organizational vocabulary. He is perceived ONLY as: a quiet old man who somehow knows precise historical terms that no one else remembers.

**Forbidden vocabulary (Manual Appendix B + chapter-specific):**  
ancient order · chosen · destiny · for centuries · secret society · Holder · Verifier · Connector · Walker · Transmitter · Covenant · Article · Gumma · civilizational memory · mystical · supernatural · steward · custodian · rememberer · keeper · guardian · protector · institution · order · network · trained · mandate · duty (institutional) · his kind · always been someone · watching · carrying this

**Eyewitness forbidden (ISS-10):**  
No "I was there" · "I saw" · "I remember when they sat" · "I watched Mallappa agree" · any phrasing implying 1962 personal presence. Terms spoken as **held memory** — "what was agreed," "what was held," "what both sides were given to remember." Ch25 "what I was told" is the later explicit model; Ch16 must not contradict it.

**Invisible layer limits:**  
**3 RP.** Function demonstrated. Absolutely NO labeling. The reveal is in WHAT he says (precise terms), not in WHO he is (which stays unknown). Prose limit on Shankarappa's actual speech: 250–350 words maximum. The rest is reaction, silence, and aftermath.

**POV restrictions:**  
Close-third Meera. She witnesses everything. She cannot know Shankarappa's interior (why he decided to speak; what it cost him). She reads his face: exhaustion, not triumph. She reads the room: silence, flinching. She has questions she doesn't ask.

**Dialogue restrictions:**  
Shankarappa's speech: calm, specific, in village-Kannada register. He uses fathers' names, the specific year, the physical marker (tamarind tree + storm), and quotes Mallappa's exact words as **held terms**, not witnessed events. He does NOT attribute source on-page.

**Ramesh:** silence (or one word: the kind of sound a man makes when his stomach drops).
**Venki:** silence (or immediate departure).
**Lakshmi:** one look. No words.
**Meera:** no dialogue.

**Watching-cost (ISS-03):** Complicity — Meera implicated; fail if observation feels detached or identical to Ch12 social-risk.

**Allowed ambiguity:**  
How he knows; why he waited; whether he's right (both fathers' inability to deny is the only proof); whether this will actually help or just humiliate; what Lakshmi knows.

## 2. Chapter Purpose

**Why this chapter exists:**  
Show shame from the inside. Ramesh processes the 1962 revelation: his "our path" narrative was wrong. His father's word — which he'd built identity around — was "let the path be wider than our pride." He has been the pride. He IS what his father warned against. This chapter is Ramesh's crack. The field-walk is his processing space. Arjun calls and says "just put it in writing" — the modern son's instinct — and Ramesh is caught between old shame and new temptation.

**Removal test (what breaks if deleted):**  
Ramesh's internal response to Ch16 is absent; P-PAPER never opens; Arjun's ideology has no scene; the pride → humiliation arc doesn't get its private moment; Ch.25 (return) has no contrition foundation.

**Promise job:**  
P-PAPER opens — the temptation to convert oral memory into legal document (which would solve nothing, as the novel will show). P-PATH continues in new phase (post-truth).

**Emotional job:**  
Shame. The deep kind that comes from discovering you were the problem you blamed on others.

**Institutional / RP job:**  
0 RP. Shankarappa doesn't appear. The residue of his words lives in Ramesh's body.

**Explicit exclusion (DEC-A / ISS-04):**  
No Meera–Ramesh quiet beat in this chapter — that beat is locked for **Ch25**. Ramesh processes alone.

---

## 3. Opening State

**POV entering emotion:**  
Woke early; couldn't eat; went to fields. Running on shame-adrenaline. Couldn't face courtyard where yesterday happened.

**POV entering knowledge:**  
Ramesh now knows: the path was always shared; his father agreed to sharing; his monopoly-claim was wrong; Shankarappa's words were too precise to deny. He hasn't spoken to anyone since yesterday evening.

**Other characters — entering emotions:**
- Arjun (phone call — Mysuru): practical, modern; heard about the meeting through family grapevine; sees a legal solution
- Saroja (offstage): worried about Ramesh; letting him be
- Venki (offstage): also processing; also humiliated (his side narrows too)
- Meera (offstage): in village; may have received second Nav ping today (ISS-11 — noted in hooks only; not Ramesh's knowledge)

**Unresolved pressure entering:**  
Ch16 truth; both families diminished; path dispute now has facts but no resolution; Ramesh's identity as "reasonable protector" shattered.

**Reader assumptions currently true:**  
Maybe written agreement will fix this; maybe Ramesh will accept paper solution.

**Reader assumptions we will not correct this chapter:**  
Whether paper can substitute for memory (novel holds this tension).

---

## 4. Scene List

### Scene B01-Ch17-S01

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Location | Fields (L15) — sugarcane; alone; walking between rows |
| Time of day | 7:00 a.m. → 11:00 a.m. |
| Characters onstage | Ramesh alone (fields + his interior) |
| Offstage pressure | Yesterday's words; family avoiding him; the courtyard he can't face |
| Goal (POV want) | Find a way to hold his self-image; process without audience |
| Conflict / obstacle | **Micro-recovery (ISS-12): field beauty without redemption** — the sugarcane rows in grey morning light are objectively beautiful: green against cloud, the particular smell of cut stalk, dew on leaves. Ramesh notices this before he notices his shame — not comfort, just the world continuing. Then the mind won't stop: "My father said: wider than our pride. I became the pride. I am what he cautioned against." The sugarcane leaves are sharp. He walks between them, occasionally snapping one. The action is mindless, physical. He tries to reframe: maybe Shankarappa is wrong? But the tamarind tree detail — he remembers that tree. It WAS there. It DID fall in '88. The specificity is inarguable. He's left with: his position was built on a story he told himself about his father's story. And the real story was: share. |
| Turning point | A sugarcane leaf edge cuts his thumb (not dramatic — the slow paper-cut type from sharp green leaf). He looks at the blood. Thinks: "Even the field wants me to feel this." Absurd thought. But the body-pain breaks the mental loop for one moment. He can start to breathe. He hasn't solved anything. But the shame has peaked and now it's just: present. Heavy. Alive. He carries it back toward the house. |
| Exit image | Cane leaf cutting his thumb; small blood; grey sky above rows — beauty and hurt coexisting, neither canceling the other |
| Emotional delta | Shame-spiral → physical break → settled heaviness |
| Reveal usage | 0 |
| Continuity dependencies | Sugarcane fields (Ramesh's crop — established Ch4); yesterday's events; tamarind tree memory (it confirms Ch16's detail — Ramesh knows that tree was real) |
| Continuity outputs | Ramesh has NOT resolved his shame; has NOT spoken to anyone; has walked fields for hours; cut thumb (minor — not injury); ready to return to house; will face Arjun's call |
| Word budget | 1,500 |
| Fail if | Ramesh becomes essay-machine; fields become symbolic lecture; shame becomes self-pity monologue; narrator explains what the institution did to him; field beauty becomes redemption or hope |

### Scene B01-Ch17-S02

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Location | Back at house — back porch; phone call with Arjun |
| Time of day | 12:30 p.m. → 3:00 p.m. (call itself: ~20 min within this block) |
| Characters onstage | Ramesh; Arjun (phone voice) |
| Offstage pressure | Arjun heard about yesterday through cousin grapevine; called to "advise" |
| Goal (POV want) | Hear his son; maybe find direction |
| Conflict / obstacle | Arjun: "Appa, if he says there were terms, put it in writing now. Get both sides to sign. Then this can't happen again. Written agreement. Survey adjustment. Legal standing." His voice is confident — the modern paper-first ideology. Ramesh hears it and: part of him wants this (certainty! paper! protection!). Part of him hears his father's voice underneath: "Let neither of us need a document for what our sons should remember." Arjun is the son. And he's saying: give me a document. The irony is crushing. |
| Turning point | Ramesh doesn't commit. He says: "I'll think." Arjun pushes: "Think about what? This is simple. Boundaries, writing, signatures." Ramesh: "It wasn't supposed to need that." The sentence comes out before he means it to — it's his father's position, surfacing through him. He didn't plan to say it. Arjun goes quiet. The call ends warmly but unresolved. P-PAPER is now live: the temptation and its refusal hang in the air. |
| Exit image | Phone put down; thumb still with dried blood line; looking at the field direction where Chandrappa and Mallappa once agreed without paper; the absence of document as both the problem and the principle |
| Emotional delta | Exposed → tempted → resisting → isolated |
| Reveal usage | 0 |
| Continuity dependencies | Arjun's character (paper-first modern; Mysuru); phone infrastructure; Ramesh's back porch (Ch4); yesterday's events known to extended family |
| Continuity outputs | P-PAPER opened as internal conflict; Arjun's position stated (write it down); Ramesh's resistance stated (wasn't supposed to need that); call ended without decision; Ramesh alone again; shame + isolation continuing |
| Word budget | 900 |
| Fail if | Arjun is straw-man for "modernity bad"; Ramesh becomes romantic about oral tradition (he's in PAIN about it, not proud); call becomes debate about tradition vs law |

**Correct interpretation (writer only):**  
- The tension between oral custody and written law is the novel's recurring rhyme. Arjun's "write it" impulse is rational but misses: the power of the 1962 agreement was precisely that it lived in PEOPLE not paper. When paper replaces memory, you get contracts. When memory replaces paper, you need custodians. The novel doesn't resolve this tension — it holds it.

## 10. Drafting Constraints

**Prose must avoid:**  
Romanticizing farming/fields; making shame a monologue; narrator sympathy-lectures; Arjun as straw-man; field beauty as redemption or hope (ISS-12); any Meera–Ramesh shared scene (DEC-A).

**Forbidden exposition:**  
Institutional explanation of Shankarappa; path history recap beyond Ramesh's interior.

All standard Appendix B. Specifically also: institution · custodian · rememberer · the kind of man · his type · keeper · why he knows

**POV restrictions:**  
Close-third Ramesh. Arjun perceived through phone voice only. Meera not onstage.

**Dialogue restrictions:**  
Arjun speaks modern-professional (clear, solution-oriented). Ramesh speaks in broken starts. Key exchange compressed — max 8 exchanges total.

**ISS-12 lock:** Field beauty registers; shame does not lift because of it.

**Allowed ambiguity:**  
Whether Ramesh will eventually call a lawyer; whether Arjun is right in a different world; whether shame will become change.

---

## 2. Chapter Purpose

**Why this chapter exists:**  
Parallel the village story with its urban mirror. Nav drafts his resignation letter — the moment where loyalty breaks and agency asserts itself. But he doesn't send it. The chapter is about the space between deciding and acting, between the draft and the send button. It rhymes with Ramesh's field-walk (processing a broken narrative) and with the novel's theme (promises that exist in the air between people, never papered or retracted).

**Removal test (what breaks if deleted):**  
Nav's arc has no crisis point; Ch.28 resolution arrives without the resignation-draft precedent; the urban-rural thematic parallel loses its midpoint; the novel's oral-promise theme loses its corporate mirror at maximum tension.

**Promise job:**  
P-ESOP advances to crisis — Nav has mentally quit but hasn't executed. The promise (ESOP) is now actively refused internally but not yet formally confronted.

**Emotional job:**  
Loneliness. The specific kind of being alone with a decision that changes everything, with no one to check it with (Meera is in the village; he's processing alone).

**Institutional / RP job:**  
0 RP. Purely civilian. Thematic rhyme only (oral promises; loyalty without instruments; the gap between what's said and what's binding). No institutional content.

---

## 3. Opening State

**POV entering emotion:**  
Saturday-evening restlessness. Meera's been gone weeks (village for wedding). Nav has been stewing since Ch.2 corridor moment. The ESOP clarity meeting never happened. Vikram postponed twice more. Nav has hit his limit.

**POV entering knowledge:**  
Nav knows: 4+ years of loyalty; promise made 2021; Series A happened 2023; equity still unpapered; Vikram's warmth is performance. He's been looking at market salaries. The gap is large. He could leave and earn more. But: the promise. The sunk cost. The team he built. Meera texted two days ago: "Things wild here. Tell you later." — he didn't push.

**Other characters — entering emotions:**  
- Vikram (offstage): oblivious; probably socializing; not thinking about Nav
- Meera (offstage): in village dealing with path drama (Ch16-17); unreachable emotionally tonight
- Nav's flat: empty; his own company

**Unresolved pressure entering:**  
Ch2 corridor (promise re-aired, dodged); months of avoidance since; Meera absent (no sounding board).

---

## 4. Scene List

### Scene B01-Ch18-S01

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Location | Nav's flat — living room; laptop on lap; Saturday night |
| Time of day | 8:00 p.m. → 10:00 p.m. |
| Characters onstage | Nav alone |
| Offstage pressure | Market offers open (he's been interviewing); Vikram's silence; team he'd leave behind |
| Goal (POV want) | Write the letter. Make it real. Decide. |
| Conflict / obstacle | He types. Deletes. Types. The letter oscillates between professional ("After careful consideration, I have decided...") and honest ("You promised me equity in a car in 2021 and I believed you and that makes me an idiot"). Neither version feels right. The professional version feels like surrender (leaving politely means accepting the theft quietly). The honest version feels like weakness (admitting he was naive). He can't find the tone. **Micro-recovery (ISS-12): warm Meera memory** — mid-frustration, he remembers a specific ordinary moment: Meera laughing at his terrible attempt to cook dal in this same flat, years ago; the smell of burnt cumin; her saying "You're hopeless but you tried." Not sentiment — just a body-memory of being known. It doesn't fix anything. It makes the loneliness sharper because she's not here. |
| Turning point | He writes a middle version — direct, not cruel: "I was told something in 2021 that shaped my next four years. It wasn't honored. I don't need you to explain why. I need to leave before the person I blame becomes me." He reads it. It's perfect. His finger hovers over send. |
| Exit image | Cursor blinking in the "To:" field (he hasn't typed Vikram's email yet); the letter is done; the send is not |
| Emotional delta | Restless determination → frustrated cycling → brief warmth-memory → clarity → threshold paralysis |
| Reveal usage | 0 |
| Continuity dependencies | Ch2 corridor; ESOP history; Meera's absence; Nav's flat (first detailed appearance — not Meera's flat); Meera's recent text |
| Continuity outputs | Letter drafted (exists as digital artifact); NOT sent; Nav at the threshold; alone; no resolution tonight |
| Word budget | 1,400 |
| Fail if | Letter becomes HR-advice lecture; Nav's startup life is explained for reader; interior monologue becomes essay on loyalty; resignation scene is dramatic (it's quiet and lonely); Meera memory becomes phone call or resolution |

### Scene B01-Ch18-S02

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Location | Same flat — kitchen; phone check; then dark |
| Time of day | 10:00 p.m. → 11:30 p.m. |
| Characters onstage | Nav alone |
| Offstage pressure | The unsent email (laptop now closed); the morning that will come; whether he'll send it Monday |
| Goal (POV want) | Make peace with tonight — either send or definitively don't |
| Conflict / obstacle | He checks phone — nothing from Vikram (of course; Saturday). Meera's last text still unread in the thread ("Things wild here. Tell you later." — two days ago). He doesn't push. He makes tea. Drinks it. Looks at the closed laptop. Thinks: "If I send this, Monday is different. If I don't, I'm the same person Tuesday morning. I'm the person who drafted and didn't send." That thought — being someone who writes and doesn't act — is worse than either option. But tonight, he's still that person. Because he's not sending tonight. He's going to sleep. And the email will be there tomorrow. |
| Turning point | He doesn't send. He goes to bed with it unsent. This is not cowardice — it's the honest state: he hasn't decided. The letter exists but hasn't entered the world. Like an oral promise that was never honored: it's real in one person's experience and invisible to everyone else. The parallel to Vikram's promise is structural (writer knows) but Nav doesn't think this thought. |
| Exit image | Unsent email on closed laptop; dark flat; the cursor blinking when he opens it tomorrow will still be in the "To:" field |
| Emotional delta | Restless → resigned → lonely → asleep with it unresolved |

| Word budget | 700 |
| Fail if | He sends it (must not — Ch28 requires negotiation not resignation); Meera calls and counsels (she's unreachable); narrator spells out the parallel to village story |

## 2. Chapter Purpose

**Why this chapter exists:**  
Comic relief with thematic function. The apartment AGM (parking, CCTV, maintenance fund) is the urban micro-version of the village path dispute — same human pattern (contested shared space; whose rules?) in a completely different costume. For the first time, Meera sees the rhyme herself: "This is the same fight. Smaller scale. Same structure." P-APT closes as truce (not victory, not friendship — pragmatic coexistence with Asha). Same-day BLR round-trip fatigue grounds the comedy in real bodily cost.

**Removal test (what breaks if deleted):**  
P-APT never closes; the urban-rural thematic rhyme never becomes conscious for Meera; the novel loses its comic-air beat at this position; reader has no breathing room between Ch18 loneliness and Ch20 new mystery; Nav warmth thread loses a natural ping window.

**Promise job:**  
P-APT closes as truce. Not resolution; coexistence. Asha and Meera will never be friends. But the parking fight is over because it's absurd — and Meera now SEES it's absurd because she's lived the village version.

**Emotional job:**  
Amusement — the healing kind. The click of recognizing a pattern across scales. Brief, warm, slightly melancholic (because seeing the pattern means she's changed).

**Institutional / RP job:**  
0 RP. Purely civilian thematic work.

---

## 3. Opening State

**POV entering emotion:**  
Bone-tired. She night-bused to Bengaluru Sunday night (arrived Mon dawn, slept three hours on the cousin's sofa). Worked a full Monday — client fire-drill, skipped lunch, back-to-back calls. Now AGM at 6 p.m. Her spine hurts from the bus seat. She wanted to skip but proxy wasn't arranged in time. Present grudgingly, running on coffee and spite.

**POV entering knowledge:**  
Meera knows: her apartment has an AGM tonight; CCTV proposal on agenda; parking rules being revised; Asha will be there (they haven't spoken since Ch1 parking humiliation). She also carries weeks of village experience (path, families, shame — she will not name these aloud in BLR). Nav texted earlier asking if she's "still alive" after the weekend — she hasn't replied yet.

**Other characters — entering emotions:**
- Asha: prepared; agenda-driven; expects opposition
- Other residents (6-8): mixed boredom and grievance
- Building manager: trying to keep meeting on schedule

**Unresolved pressure entering:**  
Village weight (background — Meera's mind is partly still there). P-APT lingering since Ch1. Return bus tonight — she must pack and leave by 8:45 or miss the night service.

**Reader assumptions currently true:**  
Apartment fight is petty and unresolved; Meera is city-competent; village arc is separate and heavier.

**Reader assumptions we will not correct this chapter:**  
Whether village and apartment governance are "the same thing" objectively — Meera feels a rhyme; narrator does not confirm.

---

## 4. Scene List

### Scene B01-Ch19-S01

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Location | Apartment meeting hall (ground floor community room) |
| Time of day | 6:00 p.m. → 8:30 p.m. |
| Characters onstage | Meera; Asha; 6-8 other residents; building manager |
| Offstage pressure | Return bus tonight; work emails; village weight; unread Nav text |
| Goal (POV want) | Survive this meeting; not antagonize Asha; go home and pack for return bus |
| Conflict / obstacle | **Human lead first:** Meera arrives late (traffic from office), finds a hard plastic chair, checks phone — Nav's second text: "Seriously ok?" She types one line between agenda items: *Alive. AGM hell. Reply later.* Sends. Puts phone away. AGM proceeds: maintenance hike (debate); CCTV in parking (debate — Meera's Ch1 incident cited as "example of why monitoring needed" by Asha without naming Meera directly); visitor parking rules revised. Meera watches the arguments with new eyes: this is governance. These are people arguing about shared space. The CCTV proposal is "who watches whom" — same structure as village path ("who owns the passage"). The maintenance fund is "who pays for what both use." She doesn't think this in institutional language — she just feels: "Oh. This is the same thing." Fatigue makes the recognition sharper, not softer — she's too tired to perform sophistication. |
| Turning point | A resident proposes a compromise on CCTV (cameras in parking only, not corridors; 6-month trial). Asha hesitates — she wanted full coverage. Another resident says: "Asha-ji, let's try six months. If it works, we expand. If not, we save money." Asha agrees. The room moves on. Meera realizes: Asha just accepted a compromise because someone framed it as time-limited and face-saving. Not because someone proved her wrong. The METHOD of resolution matters more than the content. Chairs scrape as people shift — the sound of agreement arriving not from victory but from exhaustion and formatting. |
| Exit image | Hall chairs scraping on tile as people stand; the AGM ending with murmured agreements; Meera's half-smile — amusement at recognizing the pattern through her own exhaustion |
| Emotional delta | Obligation fatigue → watchful → amused recognition |
| Reveal usage | 0 |
| Continuity dependencies | Ch1 parking incident; Asha's character (rule-bright); apartment geography; AGM format; Nav texting established Ch2+ |
| Continuity outputs | P-APT closed as truce (CCTV compromise; visitor rules updated; Meera's violation no longer active complaint); Meera–Asha never reconcile warmly but the fight is done; thematic click: "same pattern, different scale"; Nav ping sent (warm, offstage) |
| Word budget | 1,600 |
| Fail if | AGM is boring/tedious (must be comic-sharp); Meera has a dramatic epiphany (it's a half-smile, not a revelation); narrator explains the parallel (Meera FEELS it; narrator stays close-third); travel fatigue absent from body (ISS-24) |

### Scene B01-Ch19-S02

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Location | Apartment corridor → her flat (packing for return bus) |
| Time of day | 8:45 p.m. → 9:30 p.m. |
| Characters onstage | Meera alone (brief: passing Asha in corridor — one nod; not warm, not hostile) |
| Offstage pressure | Return bus tonight; village waiting; the amusement settling; legs still aching from bus + chair |
| Goal (POV want) | Pack quickly; catch bus; carry this insight back without forcing it |
| Conflict / obstacle | None dramatic. The conflict is internal and already resolved: Meera now sees that governance — at every scale — is the same problem: shared space, competing claims, face-management. The village isn't alien. Her apartment isn't sophisticated. They're the same animal. The insight doesn't make her superior — it makes her sad, amused, and slightly more humble. She packs. **Agency beat (ISS-02):** In the corridor she sees Asha. Asha looks tired too — AGM took something out of everyone. Meera could keep walking (maintain cold distance). She could offer a word (false warmth). She **chooses** the nod: neither smiles; neither scowls; neither stops. That nod IS the truce — a deliberate minimum, not accident. |
| Turning point | The nod. P-APT closes not with reconciliation but with mutual acknowledgment: we exist in shared space; we've stopped fighting about it; we'll never be friends; that's enough. Meera's choice to offer it (not wait for Asha) is the agency. |
| Exit image | Bag packed; door closing; corridor light; the nod lingering — the least dramatic resolution possible, which makes it real; bus ticket in pocket |
| Emotional delta | Amused → settling → practical → slightly melancholic (she's changed and knows it) |
| Reveal usage | 0 |
| Continuity dependencies | S01 AGM; Meera's flat (Ch1/Ch3); return bus logistics; Asha's corridor presence |
| Continuity outputs | P-APT closed; Meera departing BLR again; back to village tonight; carrying the parallel-recognition with her |
| Word budget | 600 |
| Fail if | The nod becomes a scene; Meera and Asha have a "moment"; narrator spells out what the truce means; nod reads as passive accident rather than choice |

**Scene word budget sum:** 1,600 + 600 = 2,200 ✓

## 2. Chapter Purpose

**Why this chapter exists:**  
Introduce Gayatri — the second institutional presence — through her function, not her identity. She "drops by" the Hegde house with jasmine flowers (a normal village-women thing), and in the course of a casual visit, somehow softens the mothers' mood toward Gowda-side. She doesn't mediate. She doesn't argue. She adjusts TIMING — arrives when the women are vulnerable-open (post-morning chores, tea time, no men present), brings beauty (jasmine), and creates conditions for the mothers to say conciliatory things they wouldn't have said unprompted. Meera notices: this is the second time she's seen quiet competence do work in this village. Unease. **Human-first:** morning chores and tea before any institutional texture arrives.

**Removal test (what breaks if deleted):**  
Gayatri has no introduction; the Connector function never demonstrates; the novel loses its evidence that there might be a NETWORK (Shankarappa + Gayatri = pattern, not anomaly); P-GAYA never opens; the women's sphere of conflict-resolution stays invisible; Lakshmi mid-book presence vanishes (DEC-I).

**Promise job:**  
P-GAYA opens — who is she? Why did she show up at exactly the right moment? Is she connected to Shankarappa?

**Emotional job:**  
Unease — the growing kind. Meera recognizing a pattern: two instances of quiet competence changing a room's temperature. Once (Ch16) could be a person. Twice is a pattern. The unease is: "What am I looking at?"

**Institutional / RP job:**  
**2 RP (R3).** On-page: Gayatri timing softens mothers. Reader interprets: "Helpful relative/friend." Incorrect: Random coincidence, or she's police-adjacent. Correct (writer only): Connector function — she creates conditions for de-escalation without claiming credit.

---

## 3. Opening State

**POV entering emotion:**  
Returned from BLR AGM (Ch19) slightly lighter; village routine resuming; post-Shankarappa weeks settling into uneasy normalcy. Body still carrying bus fatigue residue but mind clearer after the AGM click.

**POV entering knowledge:**  
Meera knows: Shankarappa spoke the terms (Ch16); both fathers shamed; no resolution yet achieved (but no further violence either); she's observed Ch19 parallel; she's more alert to governance patterns now. She does NOT know Gayatri (may have seen her briefly at wedding; files as "someone's aunt/friend").

**Other characters — entering emotions:**
- Gayatri: precise; warm-surfaced; internally deliberate (writer-only: she's been watching the mothers' emotional temperature for days; today is the day they're soft enough)
- Saroja: tired; morning chores done; tea time; guard down
- Revathi: present; chatty; comfortable
- Lakshmi: in her room; routine morning; opacity intact
- Venki's wife (offstage but was here yesterday — mothers have been cautiously visiting)

**Unresolved pressure entering:**  
Path still unresolved formally; police file still open; fathers still cold; but: mothers have been cautiously bridging (meal exchanges; festival prep talk). The women's sphere is doing slow peace-work without naming it.

**Reader assumptions currently true:**  
Shankarappa's Ch16 speech was singular; Gayatri if noticed is "helpful aunt type."

**Reader assumptions we will not correct this chapter:**  
Whether Gayatri and Shankarappa connect; whether Meera's pattern-suspicion is correct.

## 4. Scene List

### Scene B01-Ch20-S01

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Location | Hegde house — courtyard → kitchen (women's morning space) |
| Time of day | 10:00 a.m. → 11:30 a.m. |
| Characters onstage | Meera (present, helping); Saroja; Revathi; Gayatri (arrives ~10:30, casual, with fresh jasmine in a steel bowl); Lakshmi (passes through briefly — see S02) |
| Offstage pressure | Fathers' cold war; men away from house (fields/town); women's space is free to breathe |
| Goal (POV want — Meera) | Help with morning tasks; be present; observe |
| Conflict / obstacle | **Human lead first (ISS-07):** Meera is elbow-deep in dishwater and coriander prep when the chapter opens — Saroja dictating a shopping list, Revathi complaining about onion prices, the ordinary friction of a household morning. Tea steams. No mystery yet. Gayatri arrives as if dropping by — "I was at the temple, these were extra, thought you'd want them." Jasmine in a steel bowl. Normal village-woman gesture. She sits. Tea offered. She accepts. She asks Saroja about "the Gowda girl — Karthik's wife now — how is she settling?" It's a gentle question. Normal. But it opens a door: Saroja says "Fine, I think. Ramesh won't ask but I might send something." Revathi adds: "Let's send the coconut pickle — their side likes it." A conciliatory gesture is being born in real-time. It wouldn't have been born without Gayatri's question — but Gayatri didn't ASK them to send anything. She created the condition for them to offer. |
| Turning point | The jasmine is distributed. The conversation has softened. Gayatri says nothing political, nothing about the path. She mentioned the Gowda daughter-in-law ONCE. The rest is temple talk, weather, fabric store. But by the time she leaves, Saroja has committed to sending pickle to the Gowda house. A bridge-gesture is born. Gayatri takes no credit. She leaves with an empty steel bowl and a smile. |
| Exit image | Jasmine distributed across the house (pooja, Lakshmi's room, kitchen); Gayatri's steel bowl empty and clean; Saroja planning the pickle delivery; the room 2 degrees warmer than when Gayatri arrived |
| Emotional delta (Meera) | Domestic routine → mildly alert (who is this woman?) → watchful → unsettled (something just happened and she can barely see it) |
| Reveal usage | **2 RP (R3)** — Gayatri's timing softens mothers; creates conditions for conciliatory gesture |
| Continuity dependencies | Women's sphere of the house; Saroja's position (anxious but open); Gayatri's casual-seeming arrival; jasmine season (consistent with late April); steel bowl prop |
| Continuity outputs | Gayatri introduced (functional presence observed by Meera); pickle gesture committed; mothers softened; P-GAYA open; Meera's unease growing; jasmine distributed through house (sensory residue) |
| Word budget | 1,700 |
| Fail if | Gayatri is mysterious/atmospheric; the "manipulation" is visible/unkind; mothers are passive puppets (they CHOOSE to soften — Gayatri only created the opening); Meera sees through it completely (she suspects something but can't articulate what); chapter opens on Gayatri or pattern-talk before chores (ISS-07) |

### Scene B01-Ch20-S02

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Location | Courtyard — after Gayatri has left; Meera alone briefly |
| Time of day | 11:45 a.m. → 12:30 p.m. |

| Conflict / obstacle | Meera sits in the courtyard. The jasmine smell is everywhere. She thinks: "That woman came, talked about nothing, and something changed." Then she thinks about Shankarappa: "That man knew things no one else remembered, and something changed." Two people. Two interventions. Neither announced, neither explained. Is this a pattern? Or is she imagining it? She can't be sure. And she can't ask anyone without sounding paranoid. **Watching-cost (ISS-03): social risk** — asking the wrong person could expose her suspicion or break someone's peace. |
| Turning point | Lakshmi passes through the courtyard carrying an empty cup. Meera almost asks: "Ajji, who is Gayatri-aunty?" **Agency beat (ISS-02):** Lakshmi's face is soft today — the jasmine reached her room; her eyes hold that particular warmth that is also a wall. Meera **chooses not to ask** — not from fear of answer but from respect for Lakshmi's peace. She lets Lakshmi pass. The question stays internal. **DEC-I:** Lakshmi's look is **opacity + warmth** — present, human, unreadable; not a reveal; not a stopped sentence; just: she received the jasmine; she is glad; she will not be interrogated today. |
| Exit image | Jasmine in steel bowl (now on the courtyard ledge, empty, cleaned); Meera's question unasked; Lakshmi's back disappearing into the house; the pattern visible only from the exact angle she's sitting at — and she's not sure she's sitting there on purpose |

| Word budget | 800 |

- She's just a helpful relative/neighbor
- Random coincidence

**Correct interpretation (writer only):**  
- **R3:** Gayatri is a Connector (H1 or adjacent). Her function: create conditions for de-escalation across gender lines. She operates in the women's sphere. Her timing is not coincidence — she's been monitoring the mothers' emotional readiness for days. She takes zero credit. She never returns to check if her intervention worked (that's not her job — she creates openings; humans do the rest). She and Shankarappa are from the same organizational family but they do not interact visibly in this novel. The reader will suspect a connection but never get confirmation in Book 1.

## 10. Drafting Constraints

**Prose must avoid:**  
Making Gayatri atmospheric/mysterious in narrator voice; revealing her deliberation; making mothers' softening look manipulated (it's genuine — Gayatri only created conditions); Meera's suspicion becoming certainty; opening on institutional texture before chores/tea.

**Forbidden exposition:**  
Connector function; network; Gayatri's purpose.

**Forbidden lore / vocabulary:**  
All Appendix B. Also: Connector · timing · function · network · planned · deliberate · staged · arranged · her purpose · the real reason · she came because

**Invisible layer limits:**  
**2 RP.** Function demonstrated through action (arrival, question, jasmine), not through labeling. Gayatri's interior NEVER shown. Narrator cannot know why she's there. Only effects are visible.

**POV restrictions:**  
Close-third Meera. Gayatri entirely external (actions, words, smile). Saroja/Revathi external. Lakshmi: external only — warmth + opacity, no interior.

**Dialogue restrictions:**  
Gayatri speaks in warm village-woman register (casual, generous, unhurried). The key question ("How is Karthik's wife settling?") must feel natural — one sentence among many. Saroja responds warmly. No one discusses the path directly.

**Watching-cost constraint (ISS-03):**  
Meera's withheld question costs her **social risk** — she could look paranoid or break Lakshmi's peace. Do not merge with other chapters' watching registers.

**Allowed ambiguity:**  
Whether Meera is right about the pattern; whether Lakshmi knows Gayatri.

## 2. Chapter Purpose

**Why this chapter exists:**  
Introduce Rohan Desai (features journalist) as the external threat — someone hunting the same pattern Meera has noticed, but with intent to publish. The jatre is the setting: noise, crowd, festival. Rohan approaches Meera (he's heard she's "the city niece who's been asking around") and probes. Meera realizes: he's looking for what she half-sees. But his intent is capture/expose, not understand. She **chooses** to close the door she was opening — and doesn't fully know why. **DEC-C:** Gayatri appears silently in the crowd — scenery only; zero RP, zero dialogue, zero function; Meera may or may not notice; no conclusion; not near Shankarappa pairing.

**Removal test (what breaks if deleted):**  
Rohan has no introduction; the exposure-threat never plants; P-ROHAN never opens; the reader doesn't see that the pattern (Shankarappa/Gayatri) is visible to others too; Meera's protective instinct toward the village never develops; the jatre atmosphere (noise, crowd, festival) is lost; Gayatri second-sighting seed lost (DEC-C).

**Promise job:**  
P-ROHAN opens — will this journalist discover/expose the pattern? The threat of exposure is now active (series-level; not resolved Book 1).

**Emotional job:**  
Unease — the specific kind when someone asks you about something you were privately wondering about, and their asking makes you realize you'd rather they didn't know.

**Institutional / RP job:**  
**0 RP** by design. No institutional reveal happens. Rohan is STARVED. Gayatri is SCENERY ONLY (DEC-C). Doors close in his face. But his hunting validates that the pattern is real (attentive reader notes: if there's nothing, why are doors closing?).

---

## 3. Opening State

**POV entering emotion:**  
Festival energy; Meera attending jatre with family; crowd-comfort; mild holiday mood. Practical human lead: she was sent to buy jaggery and thread for Lakshmi's pooja shelf — errands first, festival second.

**POV entering knowledge:**  
Meera has: two data points (Shankarappa Ch16, Gayatri Ch20); growing suspicion of a pattern; no framework to name it. She's NOT actively investigating — she's living village life and noticing things.

**Other characters — entering emotions:**
- Rohan Desai: charming; curious; genuine (not villain — he really finds these patterns interesting); slightly predatory in the journalistic sense
- Malathi (teacher; at jatre — Ananya's colleague): present; introduces Meera to Rohan casually
- Gayatri (DEC-C): in crowd periphery only — if onstage, she is buying something at a stall or passing through; no interaction with Meera, Rohan, or Shankarappa
- Festival crowd: noise, commerce, devotion, distraction

**Unresolved pressure entering:**  
P-GAYA (Meera wondering); Shankarappa still in village (quiet, uninvolved since Ch16); mothers' bridge-building progressing slowly.

**Reader assumptions currently true:**  
Pattern may be real; Gayatri was one helpful visit; Shankarappa is family guest type.

**Reader assumptions we will not correct this chapter:**  
Why Meera protects the pattern; whether Gayatri's crowd presence means anything.

## 4. Scene List

### Scene B01-Ch21-S01

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Location | Jatre ground — stalls, temple, crowd |
| Time of day | 10:00 a.m. → 1:00 p.m. |
| Characters onstage | Meera; Malathi; Rohan (introduced via Malathi); festival crowd; **Gayatri (DEC-C — silent periphery only, if visible at all)** |
| Offstage pressure | Festival schedule; family obligations (Meera needs jaggery/thread for Lakshmi); Saroja expects her back by lunch |
| Goal (POV want) | Complete Lakshmi's errand list; enjoy the fair; be present |
| Conflict / obstacle | **Human lead:** Meera negotiates jaggery price at a stall, bags thread, heat on her neck — ordinary festival logistics. Malathi introduces Rohan casually ("He's doing a features piece on village dispute-resolution methods — for a Kannada magazine"). Meera's ears prick. Rohan is charming, asks easy questions first (festival, food, village life). Then: "I hear your family had that path thing? And apparently some elder spoke the actual old terms? That's amazing — do you know who he was?" The question is direct. Meera realizes: he already knows something. He's heard talk. He's hunting. She feels her body close before her mind decides to. **DEC-C beat (embedded, not scene):** At some point in the crowd — Meera may or may not register a woman with a steel bowl or familiar profile at a flower stall. **Zero dialogue. Zero function. Zero RP.** If Meera notices, she gets no conclusion — the woman is gone in the crowd before Meera can think. **Not near Shankarappa** (he is not at jatre / not paired with her). If Meera doesn't notice, chapter works identically. |
| Turning point | **Agency beat (ISS-02):** Meera **chooses** to close the door. She says: "Just an uncle-type. He knew family history. It's not that unusual here." She's lying. She KNOWS it was unusual. But something in her — she doesn't fully understand it — doesn't want this man to have it. Rohan smiles (he knows she just closed the door). "Family memory is beautiful," he says easily. "No one values it enough." She nods. He nods. The conversation moves to biscuit stalls. But the probe happened. And she declined it — actively, not passively. |
| Exit image | Rohan's smile — genuine, curious, slightly hungry; the loudspeaker distortion drowning the conversation's residue; Meera carrying the closed-door feeling without fully understanding why she did it |
| Emotional delta | Festival warmth → alert → protective instinct → confused unease |
| Reveal usage | 0 |
| Continuity dependencies | Meera's pattern-knowledge (Ch16, Ch20); Rohan introduced; Malathi connection; jatre setting; DEC-C crowd list |
| Continuity outputs | Rohan introduced; his interest stated; Meera closed the door (choice); she doesn't know why; P-ROHAN opened; Gayatri silent second sighting possible (DEC-C); he now knows Meera is a potential source who refused |
| Word budget | 1,600 |
| Fail if | Rohan is villain/predator (he's curious and professional — not evil); Meera's refusal is articulate/principled (it's instinctive choice — she can't explain it); institutional vocabulary used; Shankarappa's name given to Rohan by Meera; Gayatri speaks, looks meaningfully, or pairs with Shankarappa (DEC-C breach); Gayatri given function or RP |

Maybe she's being paranoid. But the feeling is clear: someone asking questions about this pattern felt WRONG. Not wrong because there's nothing there. Wrong because there IS something there and it might break if someone grabs at it. She can't be sure of this. It might just be: family loyalty, small-town protectiveness, or her own desire to be the one who understands it (not a journalist). All interpretations available. None confirmed. **Watching-cost (ISS-03): protectiveness/complicity** — she has now actively participated in concealment without knowing what she's concealing. |

## 2. Chapter Purpose

**Why this chapter exists:**  
Shankarappa is offered capture (uncle Subbanna tries to own/flatter him into becoming "our side's" memory-man) and he responds by LEAVING. For three days. This is the Act II ending: the mystery deepens via absence. The reader (and Meera) now understand: whatever he is, he cannot be owned. His value is demonstrated by his refusal to be instrumentalized. The empty guest mat is the image that closes Act II. **ISS-19:** Subbanna's warmth campaign pays off here (planted Ch12+). **Meera agency:** she witnesses and accepts — does not chase.

**Removal test (what breaks if deleted):**  
Shankarappa's non-extraction principle never demonstrates; the three-day absence (Ch23) has no cause; Act II has no ending; the institutional principle that custodians cannot be captured is never shown; P-ABSENCE never opens; Subbanna feels schematic without prior warmth plant.

**Promise job:**  
P-ABSENCE opens (where is he? will he return?). P-GUEST advances dramatically (he refused capture — he has principles Meera can't name).

**Emotional job:**  
Unease → abandonment. The specific dread of losing something you barely understood before it disappeared.

**Institutional / RP job:**  
**2 RP (R4).** On-page: relative tries to flatter/own; Shankarappa refuses and leaves. Reader interprets: "He's proud/offended." Incorrect: ego bruised. Correct (writer only): non-extraction principle — a Holder cannot be instrumentalized by either side or the function is corrupted.

---

## 3. Opening State

**POV entering emotion:**  
Normal morning; slightly watchful (post-Rohan, post-Gayatri); alert to patterns. Human lead: she's folding laundry on the courtyard line when the chapter opens — ordinary domestic before tension.

**POV entering knowledge:**  
Meera knows: Shankarappa is still in the house (quiet since Ch16 — he spoke the terms; has been present but uninvolved since); uncle Subbanna (~55, Hegde cousin) has been particularly warm to Shankarappa lately (**ISS-19 payoff:** extra tea since compressed week, "stay as long as you like," "we're lucky to have you" — campaign already live from Ch12 plant, not invented today).

**Other characters — entering emotions:**
- Subbanna: genuinely impressed by Shankarappa's Ch16 performance; wants to recruit/retain him as "family advisor" — doesn't understand what he's doing wrong
- Shankarappa: internal alarm; he's been tolerating the warmth but today it crosses into attempted capture; bad knee stiff this morning (**ISS-20**)
- Lakshmi: watching; knows what's happening; worried

**Unresolved pressure entering:**  
Post-Ch16 status quo; mothers softening (Ch20); Rohan hunting (Ch21); path still formally unresolved. Shankarappa has been quiet — present but passive.

**Reader assumptions currently true:**  
Shankarappa is a respected guest; his departure would be surprising.

**Reader assumptions we will not correct this chapter:**  
Where he goes; whether he'll return; childhood identity link (DEC-G).

## 4. Scene List

### Scene B01-Ch22-S01

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Location | Hegde courtyard — morning tea; Subbanna approaching Shankarappa |
| Time of day | 11:00 a.m. → 12:30 p.m. |
| Characters onstage | Meera (present but peripheral — folding laundry); Shankarappa (seated, morning tea, **rubbing bad knee — ISS-20**); Subbanna; Saroja (background) |
| Offstage pressure | Subbanna's campaign (multiple days of warmth — **ISS-19**) reaching its ask |
| Goal (POV — Meera) | Observe; she's been watching Shankarappa peripherally since Ch16 |
| Conflict / obstacle | Subbanna sits beside Shankarappa. Overly warm — the warmth that's been building for days finally arrives as claim. "Shankarappa-avare, you've done so much for this family. Stay. We want you to be comfortable. If there's anything you need — you're our man now." The word "our." Meera sees Shankarappa's face change. Not anger — not offense — something colder. A withdrawal happening in real-time behind his eyes while his mouth stays polite. He says: "I'm no one's man." It's quiet. Not dramatic. Subbanna laughs nervously: "No, no, I just meant—" Shankarappa: "I know what you meant. Thank you for the tea." He stands. Sets the cup down precisely (on the saucer's center — **ISS-21 cup precision rhyme with Ch7**). Walks to the guest room. |
| Turning point | The cup set down. The precision. The walking away. Meera watches and feels: he just said goodbye to something. Subbanna doesn't realize what happened. But Meera does — she felt the temperature drop. **Agency (ISS-02):** Meera does not follow him, does not ask Subbanna what he meant, does not chase — she **witnesses and accepts** the boundary. |
| Exit image | Shankarappa's back walking to the guest room; the teacup perfectly centered on its saucer; Subbanna still holding his own cup, confused |
| Emotional delta (Meera) | Observant → tense → recognizing a boundary crossed → unsettled |
| Reveal usage | **2 RP (R4)** — refusal of capture/ownership; demonstrates non-extraction principle |
| Continuity dependencies | Shankarappa's guest status; Subbanna's warming campaign (Ch12+ plant — ISS-19); Meera's observation position; teacup prop; guest room as his space; bad knee Ch16 |
| Continuity outputs | Shankarappa has refused ownership; Subbanna confused/stung; Meera witnessed the refusal; the precise cup-setting is the behavioral signal (same man who aligned sandals — precision as language); departure sequence initiated |

### Scene B01-Ch22-S02

He left during afternoon rest — his ordinary habit (**ISS-20:** early departure pattern; refuses second helping at lunch; minimal possessions). Saroja discovers ~5–6 p.m.: his room is empty. Chappals gone. Mat rolled. Saroja is confused: "Did someone offend him?" Lakshmi says only: "He'll be back or he won't." The sentence is too calm. Meera reads it: Lakshmi has seen this before. Not specifically with this man — but with this TYPE of departure. The one that means: you tried to own me. **Watching-cost (ISS-03): attachment** — Meera surprises herself with how much his absence feels like loss; she barely knew him; the cost is emotional attachment revealed by removal. **Agency (ISS-02):** She does not run after him; does not ask Lakshmi to explain; accepts not chasing. |

## 6. Mystery Tracker

**Questions strengthened:**  
- **Where did he go?** (Immediate)
- **Will he return?** (Urgent — reader needs him back for resolution)
- **Why did he leave after being flattered?** (Most people stay when praised — his leaving is the mystery)
- **What does Lakshmi know?** (Her calm is suspicious/beautiful)

**Questions answered:**  
- **Can he be owned?** → No. Definitively demonstrated.

**Questions intentionally ignored:**  
- Destination; childhood identity (DEC-G); whether departure is punishment.

**Incorrect interpretations encouraged:**  
- He's proud / ego bruised (wrong — it's principle, not pride)
- He's angry at Subbanna (wrong — he's protecting function, not punishing insult)
- He won't come back (he will — Ch25)
- He died or was exiled (forbidden — DEC-J)

**Correct interpretation (writer only):**  
- **R4:** A Holder cannot be captured by either side. Non-extraction is not optional. By attempting to make Shankarappa "our man," Subbanna unknowingly triggered withdrawal. Shankarappa's three-day absence will teach both sides (via deterioration, Ch23) that his presence was functional, not social. He returns (Ch25) only when conditions for non-ownership are re-established. **DEC-J:** Departure reads as work-complete / non-extraction — not death, not exile-as-punishment.

**RP beats (Reveal Map IDs):**  
- **R4** (cost 2): Refusal of capture; departure.

## 10. Drafting Constraints

**Prose must avoid:**  
Making Shankarappa theatrical; Subbanna as villain; the departure as dramatic exit (it's quiet — he leaves during afternoon rest); narrator explaining the principle; Meera chasing; death/exile rumor; forensic childhood identity link via sandals (DEC-G).

**Forbidden exposition:**  
Non-extraction principle; Article V; protocol language.

**Forbidden lore / vocabulary:**  
All Appendix B. Also: non-extraction · principle · Article · protocol · function · constitutional · duty · withdrawal · his kind · they leave when · always been this way

**Invisible layer limits:**  
**2 RP.** Demonstrated through refusal + departure. No labeling. No explanation. Only: he was offered ownership, he said no, he left.

**POV restrictions:**  
Close-third Meera. Shankarappa: external only (words, face, cup, departure, knee rub). Lakshmi: one sentence + facial reading. Subbanna: confused warmth.

**Key dialogue:**  
Subbanna: "You're our man now." Shankarappa: "I'm no one's man." / "I know what you meant. Thank you for the tea." Lakshmi: "He'll be back or he won't."

**Motif lock (ISS-21):**  
Teacup centered on saucer — rhyme with Ch7 precision; **do not underline on-page.**

**Watching-cost (ISS-03):**  
Attachment register — Meera's surprise at loss. Not social risk (Ch20) or complicity (Ch21).

**Allowed ambiguity:**  
Whether Subbanna's warmth was harmless until today; whether Meera should have intervened.

## 2. Chapter Purpose

**Why this chapter exists:**  
Prove by negative space that Shankarappa's presence was functional. During his three-day absence, rumor escalates, the path dispute heats up again (new WhatsApp forwards claiming Hegde-side is "planning retaliation"), community temperature rises. No one connects this to the quiet man's departure in thesis form — but the reader should feel: when he was here, this didn't happen. **DEC-F:** One dismissible throwaway civilian line max ("things were quieter when the guest was here"); timing/temperature carry the argument. **ISS-23:** Compressed montage with one near-miss (almost second confrontation / almost new complaint) that does NOT happen.

**Removal test (what breaks if deleted):**  
The absence has no consequence; Ch25 return has no relief-contrast; the reader never feels what the institution prevents; the "proof by absence" argument (core to the novel's structure) is lost.

**Promise job:**  
P-ABSENCE advances — the absence teaches.

**Emotional job:**  
Dread. The slow kind: watching a situation deteriorate when the invisible brake has been removed.

**Institutional / RP job:**  
0 RP. Shankarappa is absent. The institutional layer is proved by its absence — not by any present action. This is structural argument, not textual reveal.

---

## 3. Opening State

**POV entering emotion:**  
Ramesh in post-shame quiet. **Human lead (ISS-07):** Wed morning — he wakes to an already-wrong house: guest room checked by Saroja, mat rolled, no explanation that satisfies him. The guest left yesterday afternoon while Ramesh was in the back field. Ramesh registers: "the guest left." He's not tracking it as meaningful — he's too inside his own shame.

**POV entering knowledge:**  
Ramesh knows: path terms spoken (Ch16 — he was humiliated); his position was wrong; he hasn't acted on it; he's been paralyzed. Shankarappa departed **yesterday ~3:30 p.m.** (Ch22). Now: the village WhatsApp group (extended family) starts buzzing with rumors: "Hegde planning fencing"; "Gowda buying new wire"; "both sides arming" — all false, but circulating.

**Other characters — entering emotions:**
- Village rumor ecosystem: in motion; no one fact-checks; forwards multiply
- Farooq (later in compression): trying to calm people at store; failing
- Saroja: panicked ("What are people saying?")
- Venki (offstage): similarly receiving false intelligence
- Unnamed Hegde cousin or neighbor (DEC-F throwaway line voice — NOT Farooq as oracle)

**Unresolved pressure entering:**  
Shankarappa absent (since Tue ~3:30 p.m.); mothers' bridge-building fragile; police file still open; no formal resolution; vacuum.

**Reader assumptions currently true:**  
Shankarappa left because of Subbanna's warmth (Ch22); return uncertain.

**Reader assumptions we will not correct this chapter:**  
Causal link absence → rumor (felt, not stated as thesis).

The vacuum of authority (no one credible to say "this is untrue") allows rumor to fill the space. |

| Goal (POV want) | Find some way to stop this without losing more face |
| Conflict / obstacle | Every option Ramesh has is tainted: he can't deny the forwards (looks defensive); he can't confront Gowda-side (looks aggressive); he can't go to Farooq's and ask for help (Farooq is neutral but can't fix this); he can't call the quiet man back (doesn't have his number; doesn't even know where he went). The paralysis is complete. **ISS-23 near-miss:** Thu afternoon — two field-hands from opposite sides argue at the path mouth; voices rise; someone says "call Venki" / "call Ramesh"; a small crowd gathers — then the argument loses steam; both men leave; **no second shove; no new police complaint.** The near-miss makes the dread worse: it almost reignited; nothing official stopped it — it just ... didn't. Ramesh hears about it third-hand and feels sick. |
| Turning point | Farooq, in passing: "These forwards will die on their own if someone doesn't add fuel. Problem is: everyone's adding fuel because no one's saying 'enough.'" The observation is accurate and useless simultaneously — Ramesh can't be the one who says "enough" because his credibility is compromised. The person who COULD say "enough" isn't here. (Farooq is NOT the one who said "quieter when guest was here" — DEC-F.) |
| Exit image | Ramesh at his back porch (familiar from Ch4, Ch17); phone with 23 unread WhatsApp messages; the village atmosphere tighter than when the shove happened (Ch10) — words are doing what fists did, but slower and wider |
| Emotional delta | Helpless → deteriorating → settled dread |
| Reveal usage | 0 |
| Continuity dependencies | Multi-day compression; Farooq's neutrality; WhatsApp culture; Ramesh's damaged credibility; near-miss does not open P-POLICE |
| Continuity outputs | Situation worse; no resolution; Shankarappa's absence proven as functional loss; stage set for traveler signal (Ch24) and return (Ch25); P-ABSENCE at peak |
| Word budget | 1,100 |
| Fail if | Compression feels rushed/listy without concrete beats; Ramesh suddenly becomes active; Farooq solves anything; police intervene; near-miss becomes actual violence/complaint; throwaway line repeated or elevated |

## 2. Chapter Purpose

**Why this chapter exists:**  
A traveler passes through Farooq's store and delivers one sentence to Farooq in Meera's presence. The sentence is opaque (Meera hears it but can't parse its meaning: it sounds like an oblique message, not a conversation). The traveler leaves. Farooq pauses — something crossed his face — then resumes normality. Meera witnesses the ecology of non-digital communication. P-MSG opens as a series-level mystery. **Human-first:** errands and rice-bags before any network texture. **Agency:** Meera chooses not to ask Farooq.

**Removal test (what breaks if deleted):**  
The Walker function never hints; the message ecology never appears; P-MSG never opens; the novel loses its single evidence that a NETWORK exists (not just individuals); Farooq's node-function stays invisible.

**Promise job:**  
P-MSG opens — the reader glimpses a communication system. Not explained. Not resolved. Series-level.

**Emotional job:**  
Unease — the cold kind. Seeing something move through a system you can't map.

**Institutional / RP job:**  
**1 RP (R5).** On-page: traveler delivers one sentence to Farooq; Farooq pauses; traveler leaves. Reader interprets: "Gossip network" or "conspiracy courier." Incorrect: thriller-courier; confirmed spy. Correct (writer only): Walker function — message ecology. One node to another. Content unclear by design.

---

## 3. Opening State

**POV entering emotion:**  
Errand-mode. Meera went to town for supplies (Saroja's list — rice, dal, soap). Normal Saturday morning. Stopped at Farooq's because his store is reliable and he doesn't gossip *to her face*. Background: rumor-heat from Ch23 reached her as household tension, not as investigation.

**POV entering knowledge:**  
Meera is NOT expecting anything unusual. She's at Farooq's buying provisions. Shankarappa still absent (Day 4). She has background awareness of the rumor-heat but is not investigating.

**Other characters — entering emotions:**
- Farooq: normal shop-morning; running his store
- Traveler (~50; nondescript; canvas bag; dust on shoes suggesting long bus travel): arrives, speaks, leaves
- Town bustle: background

**Unresolved pressure entering:**  
Shankarappa absent (Day 4); rumors peaked (Ch23); Meera's pattern-recognition active but undirected; Ch25 return imminent (reader expectation).

**Reader assumptions currently true:**  
Three data points forming (Shankarappa, Gayatri, ?); Shankarappa may return soon.

**Reader assumptions we will not correct this chapter:**  
What the traveler's sentence means; whether Farooq is institutional.

---

## 4. Scene List

### Scene B01-Ch24-S01

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Location | Farooq's store (L12) — Meera inside buying; bus stand (L13) visible from door |
| Time of day | 9:30 a.m. → 10:15 a.m. |
| Characters onstage | Meera (buying rice/provisions); Farooq (behind counter); Traveler (enters briefly) |
| Offstage pressure | Rumor heat; Shankarappa's absence; normal town commerce; Saroja's list half-complete |
| Goal (POV want) | Complete errand; exchange a few words with Farooq (she likes him from Ch9); leave |
| Conflict / obstacle | **Human lead (ISS-07):** Meera counts rice bags, checks Saroja's list on phone, argues lightly about dal brand — ordinary town errand friction. Normal transaction. Meera and Farooq are mid-banter ("City girl buying our rice now, ah?") when a man enters. ~50, nondescript, canvas bag with dust. He waits until another customer leaves. Then says one sentence to Farooq — clearly, not whispered, but in a register that is: meant for him. The sentence: something like "The tamarind cutting went well; he should know the cart is ready" — mundane-sounding, but with a precision that feels rehearsed. One sentence. Farooq's face does a thing: a micro-pause. Eyes flick to the sentence-giver. A nod. "Good." One word. The traveler doesn't buy anything. Doesn't sit. Turns and walks toward the bus stand. |
| Turning point | Meera saw all of it. The sentence made no conversational sense in context. There was no tamarind cutting happening locally (the tree fell in '88 — she heard this in Ch16). The word "cart" — the path was measured in cart-widths (Ch16). She can't be SURE it's connected. But her body tells her: that was a message. Not gossip. Not conversation. A communication from A to B. And Farooq received it without surprise — which means he's received them before. |
| Exit image | The traveler walking toward the bus stand; dust on his bag already blending with town dust; already anonymous; already gone within 90 seconds of arriving. Farooq back to normal, wrapping Meera's rice, as if nothing happened. |
| Emotional delta | Errand-casual → alert → cold unease → alone with what she saw |
| Reveal usage | **1 RP (R5)** — message ecology hinted |
| Continuity dependencies | Farooq's store (Ch9); bus stand adjacent; Ch16 tamarind/cart references (Meera remembers these); traveler as new character type |
| Continuity outputs | P-MSG open; Meera witnessed non-digital communication; Farooq's node-role glimpsed; traveler gone (never returns in Book 1 — series); Meera cannot follow up (traveler already gone; Farooq won't explain); pattern now has THREE data points (Shankarappa, Gayatri, now a message system) |
| Word budget | 1,200 |
| Fail if | The sentence is too clear/decodable for reader; Farooq explains anything; Meera confronts Farooq; the traveler is dramatic/memorable in appearance; narrator explains the exchange; thriller pacing; chapter opens on network before errand logistics |

### Scene B01-Ch24-S02

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Location | Bus stand area — Meera leaving; looking for the traveler (gone) |

She can't ask Farooq (it would expose that she noticed). She's left holding: a sentence that sounded like code, a shopkeeper who received it normally, and a feeling that she's seeing the edges of something that is NOT hers to see. **Watching-cost (ISS-03): complicity/shame** — to ask Farooq would expose that she was watching; would drag him into her suspicion; would make her a grabber (Ch21 instinct reinforced). |
| Turning point | **Agency beat (ISS-02):** Meera **chooses not to ask Farooq.** Not out of fear — out of the same instinct from Ch21 (protecting what she half-sees). If she grabs at it, it might break. If she waits... she doesn't know. But grabbing is wrong. She walks back with her rice. |
| Exit image | Bus stand empty of the traveler; dust settling; the bag he carried already indistinguishable from every other bag on every other bus; the anonymity of the system is its architecture |

## 2. Chapter Purpose

**Why this chapter exists:**  
Shankarappa returns. He restates the terms — AND admits uncertainty: "This is what I was told. I believe it is accurate. I cannot prove it beyond my telling." This admission (judgment humility) is MORE powerful than his Ch16 confidence — because it reveals: he CHOSE to carry imperfect memory as responsibility, not as weapon. He refuses the hero role. Meera witnesses someone exercise authority while admitting limits. **DEC-A:** Meera and Ramesh share one quiet beat — shared silence/presence after return; NO path talk; NO apology; NO naming Shankarappa; earns Ch30 warmth; NOT a three-way conference with Shankarappa.

**Removal test (what breaks if deleted):**  
Shankarappa never returns; P-ABSENCE unresolved; the distinction between confidence and humility-in-function never demonstrates; Ch16 reveal is the ONLY institutional moment (too thin for novel arc); reader never sees power that admits doubt; Ch30 Meera–Ramesh nod unearned (ISS-04).

**Promise job:**  
P-ABSENCE closed (he's back). P-GUEST advanced to deepest point (he admits limits — this is more mysterious/admirable than confidence would be).

**Emotional job:**  
Disappointment — but the kind that transforms into respect. He refuses to be the answer. He refuses to be certain beyond what memory can hold. This disappoints anyone wanting a savior — and elevates anyone wanting truth.

**Institutional / RP job:**  
**2 RP (R6).** On-page: returns + admits uncertainty while restating. Reader interprets: "He's careful." Incorrect: weak/unsure/fake. Correct (writer only): judgment humility — the hardest institutional virtue. A Holder must carry AND doubt simultaneously.

---

## 3. Opening State

**POV entering emotion:**  
Morning freshness (rain came overnight — first pre-monsoon shower). The village feels lighter. Meera wakes to wet earth smell. Something has shifted. **Human lead:** she's looking for the umbrella Saroja said was behind the cot — ordinary morning logistics before she sees him.

**POV entering knowledge:**  
Meera knows: Shankarappa has been gone 4–5 days; rumors peaked (Ch23); she saw the traveler message (Ch24); she couldn't sleep well knowing the pattern exists but not understanding it. She does NOT know Shankarappa is back yet.

**Other characters — entering emotions:**
- Shankarappa: returned before dawn (his pattern — quiet arrival); seated on courtyard veranda; tea in hand; bad knee stiff from travel (**ISS-20**)
- Lakshmi: already seen him; already put out tea; calm (she knew he'd return)
- Ramesh: not yet aware (in back rooms); will emerge mid-chapter
- Saroja: will discover Shankarappa first after Lakshmi

**Unresolved pressure entering:**  
Rumor heat cooling? Path still unresolved; Ramesh's shame; families still brittle.

**Reader assumptions currently true:**  
Return expected after Ch24 signal; he won't explain where he went.

**Reader assumptions we will not correct this chapter:**  
Where he was; childhood identity (DEC-G).

| Location | Hegde courtyard veranda — morning |
| Time of day | 8:00 a.m. → 9:30 a.m. |
| Characters onstage | Meera; Shankarappa (seated, tea, **knee rubbing — ISS-20**); Lakshmi (passing briefly) |
| Offstage pressure | The rumors; the village heat; the families; Ramesh's shame |
| Goal (POV want — Meera) | Understand his return; does this change things? |
| Conflict / obstacle | Meera sees him — surprise, then relief she didn't expect to feel. He's just sitting there. Tea. Rubbing his bad knee (she noticed this detail in Ch16 — the knee is worse now; he traveled on it). He doesn't look at her directly but he doesn't avoid either. There's no announcement of return. No "I'm back." He's simply: present again. As if leaving and returning are both within his ordinary vocabulary. **Agency beat (ISS-02):** Meera **chooses to sit/wait** nearby — not next to him, but in earshot. Not asking. Waiting. This is new for her — she's learning: with this man, you don't grab. You wait. **Watching-cost (ISS-03): patience** — the cost of not grabbing is time and vulnerability; she sits in public view doing nothing, which for her is unusually hard. |
| Turning point | After a long silence, he says — not to her specifically, but she's the only one close enough to hear: "The rain came. Good." Then: "I was thinking about what I said." Meera holds still. "The terms I spoke — they are what I was told. I believe they are accurate." Pause. "I cannot prove them beyond my telling. Chandrappa and Mallappa are dead. The tree is gone. I carry what was given to me. It may be imperfect." He rubs his knee. That's it. That's the entire admission. Not weakness. Not retraction. Just: honest limitation. |
| Exit image | Him rubbing his bad knee; the admission hanging in wet air; Meera's understanding shifting — this is not a man with documents. This is a man carrying memory and saying: "I might be wrong. I'm offering it anyway." |
| Emotional delta (Meera) | Surprised relief → waiting → receiving → shifted understanding → disappointment that transforms to respect |
| Reveal usage | **2 RP (R6)** — returns; restates + admits uncertainty |
| Continuity dependencies | Ch22 departure; Ch16 terms; bad knee (Ch16 S03 detail); rain (first pre-monsoon — per timeline); Meera's waiting behavior (learned from Ch21, Ch24) |
| Continuity outputs | P-ABSENCE closed (he's back); R6 spent (humility as deeper power); Meera's understanding: he's not omniscient — he's faithful to imperfect memory, and says so; the terms stand but with admitted doubt; village stabilizing by presence (felt, not announced) |
| Word budget | 1,500 |
| Fail if | He apologizes for leaving (he doesn't — he exercises autonomy); Meera asks questions (she waits); the admission sounds weak (it must sound STRONGER for being honest); he explains where he went (he doesn't — ever); narrator celebrates the admission as institutional virtue; Meera grabs/interrogates (ISS-02) |

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Location | Back porch / courtyard edge — **DEC-A beat location** (NOT veranda with Shankarappa) |
| Time of day | 10:00 a.m. → 12:00 p.m. |
| Characters onstage | Meera; Ramesh (**DEC-A — Meera–Ramesh only**); Shankarappa visible at distance on veranda OR inside — NOT participating in this beat |

Does NOT speak to him. Goes toward the porch where Meera is checking laundry or sitting with tea. He stops. She stops. They don't plan this. **DEC-A quiet beat:** Shared silence. Maybe both looking at wet courtyard, or rain dripping off the tile edge. **NO path talk. NO apology. NO naming Shankarappa.** The silence says: we survived the same weather while he was gone. Ramesh might pour tea for her without comment, or simply stand beside her for forty seconds before going inside. Meera **chooses to stay** in the silence rather than fill it — second agency beat. This earns Ch30 "come sooner next time" — not comfort, but coexistence. **NOT a three-way conference** — Shankarappa remains separate, distant, on veranda or in guest room doorway, uninvolved in their silence. |
| Turning point | Meera observes the house atmosphere: his return is a stabilization. The rumors will start dying. Not because he'll do anything about them — but because his presence reminds people: someone is watching who knows the truth. The temperature cools by his being there. Nothing more. She thinks: "What kind of authority works by sitting?" She has no answer. But she's no longer asking "who is he?" — she's now asking "what IS this?" A harder, better question. |
| Exit image | Shankarappa rubbing his bad knee on the veranda (distant); Meera and Ramesh having already separated without goodbye; courtyard returning to ordinary rhythm; the village already slightly calmer (felt, not announced) |

| Word budget | 1,100 |
